ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u20	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the draft resolution of the Polish Sejm on the 50th anniversary of Marc.68. On the occasion of the upcoming 50th anniversary of the protests of students, students of secondary schools and young workers from March 1968, the Polish Sejm expresses respect for all those fighting for freedom and democracy at the time, condemns the communist organizers of anti-Semitic persecution and thanks for solidarity with the persecuted. March 68, which began with protests against the photo from the scene of the National Theatre 'Dziady' in the stage of Kazimierz Dejmek's performance, was an opposition to totalitarian dictatorship, censorship and political persecution. The authorities responded by launching repression against the demonstrators, launching a wave of anti-Semitic purges and press chase. Communists used a whole series of lies to make public acts of hatred in the media. There have also been brutal actions by the special services of the police state: arrests, political processes, mass dismissals and university relocations. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, remembering the dramatic fates of Polish Jews - forced by the communist authorities to leave Poland in 1968, expresses strong opposition to any symptoms of anti-Semitism. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland solidaritys with Polish citizens, regardless of their nationality and religion, living in Poland and abroad, persecuted by the communist regime and expresses the highest respect for the courage and dedication of those who have engaged in the fight against communism. Thank you. [[The gathered get up, give a round of applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u248	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland on the establishment of the National Day of Remembrance of Poles saving Jews during World War II. The Committee on Culture and Media, following its first reading and a very thorough examination of the above bill at its meetings on 25 January and 6 February, introduced - after I have already mentioned a long discussion - some amendments, which mainly concerned the introduction of the change, as regards the day to be celebrated as the National Day of Remembrance of Poles saving Jews, because we thought that the most appropriate day would be 24 March, because it was on 24 March that the Germans murdered the 9-member family of Ulm in Subcarpathia: Józef and Victoria and their seven children, as well as their hidden Jews, eight Polish citizens of Jewish nationality. In addition, the committee considered that the provision on the location of these events should be clarified by introducing the wording: under German occupation. As a result, the committee, following a vote of 16 votes in favour, with two votes against and one abstention, decided to ask the High Sejm to adopt this draft law. The act proposed by the Committee on Culture and Media shall read as follows: The Act on the establishment of the National Day of Remembrance of Poles saving Jews under German occupation. In homage to Poles - heroes who, in an act of heroic courage, extraordinary bravery, compassion and solidarity between people, faithful to the highest ethical values, commands of Christian mercy and ethos of sovereign Poland, the Republic of the Polish Friends rescued their Jewish neighbors from the Holocaust planned and implemented by German invaders - are as follows: Art. 1. On 24 March the National Day of Remembrance of Poles saving Jews under German occupation is established. Art. 2. The National Day of Remembrance of Poles saving Jews under German occupation is a national holiday. Art. 3. The Act shall enter into force on the day following that of its publication. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u252	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! This bill refers to the history of Poles, Jews, Germans and many other European nations. It concerns the history of World War II. We have the impression that fragments of this story are not fully studied and not fully known to all Poles, and this bill proposed by your President concerns this issue. Because if the Holocaust, the genocide carried out by the Germans on Jews during World War II, speaks of the whole world, in many parts of the world it was introduced, according to the so-called. the lie of Oświęcim, the punishment for for forging history and hiding real perpetrators, is about the history of those who, under the German occupation, rescued Jewish people, we know actually very little. Individual names appear in the public space, the name of Ulmów has already been mentioned here, including Kowalski, of course Irena Sendlerowa, Zofia Kossak-Szczucka, Witold Pilecki, Jan Karski, Polish ambassador Konstanty Rokicki and Henryk Sławik, but these names known to Poles and the international community should be much, much more so that the real context of what was happening in Europe could be known. It must be remembered that it was the Germans who planned, organized and carried out genocide on European Jews, that they created the machine of death, which led to the murder of about 6 million Jews. Fortunately, the Germans and their allies failed to murder all the Jews in Europe. It was thanks to attitudes such as those we are talking about in this bill that we managed to save these people. Then they could at least partially testify to what was happening in Europe. We owe these heroes a memory today because it's hard to name them differently. My predecessor said it wasn't easy. It is necessary to remind us in what circumstances the saving of Jews in Polish lands was carried out at that time. The Germans throughout Europe, of course, tried to successfully carry out the genocide plan, but only on occupied Polish and Ukrainian lands they introduced a record which said that anyone who helps people of Jewish origin in any way, even by giving a slice of bread, giving water, hiding or other actions to save them, is subject to the death penalty. This was true not only of his own kind, but also of his family, and often of his neighbors, who lived with him, for example. in a tenement. The Ulm family, which I mentioned and which is most often associated with such a heroic attitude today, experienced it on her own. Ulms and minor children stayed along with the Jews they were hiding, murdered by the Germans. This drama shows how the fate of Poles and Jews got together. True, Poles were not to be immediately exterminated, as was the case with the Jews, the Roma, but were to be brought into the role of subhumans and treated in a particularly brutal way. This bill is especially needed now. Contrary to what my predecessor said, today it seems worth talking openly about how many Poles got involved in saving Jews despite this terrible situation, despite the oppression of the German occupation. This was partly within the Polish Underground State, because this was the service of the Council of Aid to Jews of Zegota, but also of many Poles who were not institutionally involved in the activity of underground Poland with the risk of their own life, their own family provided this assistance. Today historians estimate that such heroes were hundreds of thousands, because in order to save at least tens of thousands of Jews in the Polish lands occupied by the Germans, this network of help must have included at least a few people in each individual case, because the holding of Jews did not last one day or one week, it was years when they had to be helped. No single person was able to perform such a task. So today, by finally paying the debt to those Polish heroes who saved their fellow Jews, we recognize that they deserved it. [[♪ The bell ♪]] part from the Polish state. The Law and Justice Club will therefore support the adoption of this law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u260	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The history of the world does not know heroism comparable to the heroism of Poles saving Jews. Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, with his own extraordinary nobility, repeatedly argued that Poles, saving Jews, saved their brothers. In the last census carried out before the Second World War made in 1931 only 12% Jews who were Polish citizens as their first language were mentioned in Polish. For 79% Jews, Polish citizens, the basic language was Yiddish, and for 9% - Hebrew. On the scale of Europe, this was an event, since the vast majority of the French Jews mentioned French as their language, and even a larger percentage of German Jews - German. It was similar in other countries. The data of the last pre-war census entitles us to conclude that the overwhelming majority of Polish Jews were poorly integrated with Polish society, but for centuries they came to Poland because they lived better here than in other European countries. Despite the fact that so few parts of the Jewish community felt close to Poland and Poles, something unimaginable occurred during the Nazi occupation. In 1939, the Germans transformed the whole of Poland into one great concentration camp, in which every German had the right to kill any number of Poles with impunity every day. Many Germans have exercised this right. Poles in occupied Poland had really no right to anything other than the right to die. And yet, in this hell of concentration camp, which was occupied by the Germans Poland, in which German extermination camps were only carers of one great German concentration camp, there were tens of thousands of heroes, which the world had never seen before and never since, tens of thousands of people so noble, which were not in any other country occupied by the Germans of Europe. It is said that Poles have several thousand trees in the garden of Yad Vashem, but there is no, as the Prime Minister said, one big tree for the Polish people, because it was Poles who saved the great majority of the survivors in the face of the threat of their lives and their loved ones. In Western European countries occupied by the Germans, prisons or arrests were punishable for helping Jews. In Poland, not only families with children in cradles, but also villages were murdered for giving the Jew a glass of water, giving a piece of bread or help in hiding. The Polish Righteous Among the Nations is something disproportionately greater than simply helping a neighbor. The fact that despite such an unimaginable scale of consequences there were so many of the greatest heroes in the history of the world in Poland, best speaks about what the height of heroism reached tens of thousands of Poles during the last war. Putting the lives of all their families, their children, their wives, sisters, brothers, mothers, fathers, for people who are culturally and religiously separate, others, as our fellow countrymen sometimes said about Polish Jews and added: others, but ours - did there not exist in the history of the world greater heroism? Do any other nation, any other nation, know of similar cases? The memory of these greatest heroes is all the more necessary because most of them do not remember the survivors of the nation. So let us remember, and the National Day of Remembrance of Poles saving Jews during World War II will be an expression of this. The Free and Solidarni Circle will support the bill. Thank you. [[Applause]]

[[Adjournment from 10 a.m. 17 min. 51 hrs. 17 min 55]] I'm reopening the session. We proceed with the examination of item 27 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on registration of yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long (print No 2261). I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation of Mr Jerzy Materna to present the justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u350	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill on the registration of yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long, print no. 2261. This project was created in response to a number of problems identified by the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation in the area of registration and information on small vessels. The most important condition to be taken into account when assessing the validity of the proposed changes is the protection of human life and health. At present, there is no electronic register in Poland where rescue services could confirm the data of the yacht after receiving a call for assistance, which often extends the time to reach the victims. Neither do law enforcement or state administration have access to complete data on vessels. Another problem is that the existing rules on boat registration and other vessels up to 24 m long are complicated and opaque. There are now six different registers in which inland and sea yachts can be registered. These registers are kept by the Polish Sailing Union, the Polish Motor and Water Ski Association, seniors, sea chambers and maritime offices. Detailed rules on registration obligations, as well as the parameters of the entities subject to mandatory registration and registration procedures, the documents and fees required are different in each case. This state of affairs has no substantive justification and is detrimental to both yacht owners and shipowners as well as to the administration. The largest number of vessels is registered in 380 Polish Old Towns as vessels used for amateur fishing. The registration in the Old Towns shall be open to shipowners of inland vessels without mechanical propulsion with a product of length and width exceeding 20 m2 or with a mechanical drive of a power not exceeding 20 kW. The owners of yachts and boats choose registration in the old towns due to a simple and fast procedure, low costs and easy access to the registration point. According to data collected by the Ministry in December 2016 and January 2017, more than 300 000 are entered in the registers kept by starosts. units. It should be assumed that some of these units no longer exist but were not registered by the owners. In developing the concept of registration reform, the advantages of existing solutions were sought to be taken into account, making changes where current solutions are not working. Currently, applications for registration are not harmonised between the ancients and contain very limited information insufficient to fully identify the vessel and the owner. There is no central database of information about registered vessels. In practice, there are no mechanisms to effectively verify the data contained in the application for registration and the right of the person to register the floating equipment concerned. This also means that it is not possible to use the data collected during the registration of the unit by emergency services and law enforcement authorities, e.g. in the case of theft. The oldities do not issue a registration document, but merely a registration certificate, which is not a confirmation of ownership. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of these inconveniences, inconsistencies in existing solutions, the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Shipping has proposed a number of new solutions. First of all, a single register will be created for all yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long - including inland and sea vessels, sailing and motor craft, including those intended for fishing - with the possibility to register in the Polish ship register kept by the Chambers of the Sea, which will enable the establishment of a maritime mortgage. The registration obligation will apply to all yachts and other vessels with a length of at least 7,5 m or an engine power of at least 15 kW. Smaller units can be registered at the request of the owner. The registration system will be as simple and user-friendly as possible. Registration will take place in the Old Towns and Polish sports unions: the Polish Sailing Union and the Polish Motor and Water Ski Association, in order to provide citizens with easy access to registration points and a parallel possibility to submit applications electronically. The register will be kept in the form of an electronic system to allow continuous access to data, in particular to emergency services and national administrations. The registry administrator will be the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin. One model registration document and one model registration application will be introduced. The registration document will have a simple and comfortable form of plastic card. It shall contain data on the basic parameters of the craft and the information necessary to conduct safe navigation on the vessel concerned. Other data, including voluntarily provided for the convenience of the recipients - e.g. the telephone number in emergency situations or the backboard engine number from the data plate - will be available in the electronic database and can be updated at the request of the owner via ePUAP. Mechanisms will be put in place to prevent the re-legalisation of stolen equipment by requiring the marking on the hull, the collection of data on the identification number, the presentation of a document confirming ownership during registration or the information on disposal. All existing registration documents will be replaced by new ones. To date, the paper records will remain with its maintainers, who will be obliged to maintain the archive records for 5 years after the planned completion of the exchange of documents, thus avoiding costs associated with the acquisition of registers. Records and registration documents issued under existing legislation shall remain valid. Thus: for a period of 12 months from the date of entry into force of the Act, in the case of entries in registers made and documents issued until 1 January 2000, for a period of 18 months from the date of entry into force of the Act, in the case of entries in registers made and documents issued until 1 January 2006, for a period of 30 months from the date of entry into force of the Act, in the case of entries in registers made and documents issued after 1 January 2006. As regards registration fees and their amount, this will include a regulation by the Minister for Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation. The fee for issuing the registration document is expected to be PLN 60. In the case of persons who have registered units and the provisions of the Act require them to be reregistered, the fee will be reduced by half and will amount to PLN 30. The fee will constitute respectively the income or revenue of the registration authority - local government units, i.e. the oldities, or Polish sports unions, subject to the need to transfer 10 PLN to the state budget. The contribution paid to the State budget will finance the functioning of the register in the form of an electronic system. Revenue from registration fees is expected to allow local authorities and sports unions to cover all expenditure related to the need to provide services to citizens. In order to register the unit and obtain proof or certificate, costs must be borne in mind. And so: in the Polish Sailing Union from 60 to 375 PLN depending on the size of the unit, in the Polish Motor and Water Ski Association from 60 to 150 PLN - higher fees are charged for issuing the document within 5 days - and in the old age 17 PLN. It is worth noting that the maximum limit of expenditure from the state budget for the construction and implementation of the electronic system for the registration of yachts and other vessels up to 24 m is 3560 thousand, including 390 thousand. in 2018 and 3170 thousand PLN in 2019 The Act will enter into force on 1 January 2020, with the exception of Article. 26 and Article 26 30 concerning the construction of the IT system and the expenditure rule for the years 2018 and 2019, which will enter into force after 14 days from the date of the announcement. The proposed law is compatible with European Union law. We have also prepared draft regulations which are attached to the project. They shall relate to Article. 13, Art. 14, Art. 17 and Article 17 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council In conclusion, the rules on registration of yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long will be simplified and ordered and registration will be simple, fast and cheap. The register will be kept in electronic form and will provide permanent access to data to emergency services, law enforcement and state administration. The registration document will have one model and will at the same time prove Polish nationality and the ownership of the unit. I ask the High House to give this government bill a favourable consideration. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u351	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Jerzy Wilk, the Law and Justice Club, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u352	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Law and Justice Club welcomed the government's initiative to develop the concept of a new yacht registration system and other vessels up to 24 m long. We consider the existing legislation in this area to be inconsistent, complex and opaque. There are currently six different registers where such entities are registered and detailed regulations concerning registration obligations, procedures, required documents and fees are different in each case. The draft Act on the Registration of Yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long assumes that from 1 January 2020 a single register of yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long will be created in place of the present six, and the registration process will be significantly simplified and re-bureaucratized. The register shall be kept in the form of an electronic system. The register will be accessible to government and local authorities and other entities to the extent necessary to carry out the tasks set out in the legislation, e.g. Maritime Search and Rescue Service - SAR. According to the project, units of at least 7,5 m length and engine power of at least 15 kW will be required to be registered. Smaller units not subject to registration may be entered in the register at the request of the owner. An application is created to allow verification of basic data about the unit, provided that the registration number, identification number and date of the last registration are indicated. It will allow you to check data such as name, home port, number of owners, name of registration authority, classification of the unit as inland or seaside and as recreational, commercial or fish catch, make and model, type and power drive, basic dimensions and parameters, main hull material, year of construction and possible report of theft. In practice, access via the application will only be possible to the owner and persons to whom he/she makes the relevant data available and will not include personal data. An application will also be created to allow the owner to provide additional information to emergency services. When registering, the owner will receive a unique code to log in. This will allow him to send information that will be attached to the individual's records with the date of transmission. The emergency services will be able to read and use this information when carrying out rescue operations. The planned facilitations in the procedures will include, inter alia, there is no need for prior approval of the name of the sea yacht in the Polish Sailing Union or the Maritime Office. The whole process will take place in the old age or sports relationship, depending on the choice of the owner of the unit. In order to ensure the completeness and up-to-date of the data in the register, the owner will be required to send information on the disposal and the change of the data. In addition, the Office for Electronic Communications and the Civil Aviation Office will be required to provide information on the registration of radio equipment and the Police will have to provide information on the reports of theft and discovery. Each unit shall have a unique number that will identify it throughout its life cycle, regardless of the change in the registration number and all other data. The requirements for maritime and inland vessels are now very diverse. In order to harmonise them, the draft law amends the IWT Act. Definitions of recreational and commercial yacht are introduced. With regard to the obligation of technical reviews, it will be abolished for recreational yachts with engine power exceeding 75 kW and introduced for commercial yachts with a length of at least 7,5 m or engine power of at least 15 kW and having more than 10 years. We hope that the bill will meet with a positive acceptance of the sailing environment. We hope that the solutions adopted will reduce the sense of the nuisance of registration of individuals. The exchange of documents for new documents will take place in stages within 30 months, until 1 July 2022. Discounts in fees and procedural facilitations will be applied. The owners will have the opportunity to keep POL numbers of their yachts. Applications may be submitted by electronic means. As a result of the changes made, one functional and modern register will be created, which will serve the next generations of sailors, as well as the state administration and services responsible for water safety in carrying out their statutory tasks. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u362	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I would like to present the position of the Freedom and Solidarni Circle on the Act on Registration of Yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long. Today in Poland we do not have one central register of vessels. In return, there are six different registers in which inland and sea yachts can be registered. They are run by the Polish Sailing Union, the Polish Motor and Water Ski Association, the county old settlements, maritime chambers and maritime offices. In addition, these registers, which are kept by seniors, collect different data in each district, thus containing very limited information, insufficient to fully identify the individual and the owner. This in practice leads to the fact that it is difficult to effectively verify the data contained in the application for registration and the right of the person concerned to register the floating equipment concerned. Services that are responsible, among others. for the operation of rescue operations, and law enforcement agencies have no current and complete data. The oldies issue not a registration document, but only a registration certificate, which is not a confirmation of ownership. In view of the significant difficulties we face today, a single register will be created under this Act for all yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long. This register will cover inland and sea, sailing and motor vessels, including those intended for fishing. This applies to all yachts and other vessels with a length of at least 7,5 m or an engine power of at least 15 kW. It will be a good solution to keep a register in the form of an electronic system to allow continuous access to it 24 hours a day. This will be useful for emergency services and state administration. The registry administrator will be the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin. Administration of the Szczecin system is a good solution. A large part of Polish vessels operate in the Western Pomerania. In addition, any move leading to the decentralisation of offices should be commended. The registration document will have a simple and comfortable form of plastic card, which should not cause significant difficulties for users. The registration obligation shall not apply to vessels intended solely for regatta and sports competitions, having a race class, vessels powered solely by the power of human muscles, surfboards, sailboards or other similar vessels. By creating new rules, it was right to assume that procedures should be as simple as possible and that above all they should not vary according to the Polish region. Nor should there be differentiation in requirements for small maritime and inland vessels. These requirements have so far differed considerably. A significant proportion of vessels operate in both maritime and inland waters. There are also such waters as the Szczeciński Zalew, which are treated as regions of both maritime and inland navigation. According to Art. 4 of the Act the owner will be able to register a vessel in any body (in old age, Polish Sailing Association, Polish Motor and Water Ski Association). It will be able to do so by submitting a request to the Authority's premises or by electronic means. This will make it much easier for citizens to apply formal procedures. The new rules will benefit from ensuring the safety of navigation, the protection of property rights and the efficiency of rescue operations carried out, so the Free and Solidarnia Circle will vote in favour of the law on registration of yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long.

A Polish vessel shall be required to be entered in the administrative register of Polish inland waterway vessels, hereinafter referred to as "the register', subject to paragraph 1. 2. 2. A Polish ship used solely for sport or recreation, except for units powered solely by the power of human muscles, with a hull length exceeding 12 m or with a mechanical power of more than 15 kW subject to registration of vessels used solely for sports or recreational purposes. Ships exempt from registration may be entered in the register at the request of the owner. 3. The rule of the mouth. 1 shall not apply to a vessel used for fishing without mechanical propulsion with a product of length and width not exceeding 20 m2 or with a power of up to 20 kW. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 IWT Act - i.e. the obligation to register was only from the length of the hull above 12 m. As a result of the adoption of the new law, the registration obligation will be from 7.5 m in length. ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u365	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Club the Civic Platform of the Republic of Poland the position of the club vis-à-vis the government project concerning the creation of a uniform register of yachts and other vessels up to 24 m long. The existing yacht register system is dispersed, divided into six different registers, and is therefore inconsistent and opaque and creates problems in the functioning of individual registers. The Civic Platform Club therefore sees the need to change this state of affairs. The project concerns inland and maritime, sailing and motor craft, including those intended for fishing, with the possibility of registration in maritime chambers in the case of maritime mortgages. A long wait for the issue of registration documents and the lack of reliable information on the fleet, in particular the lack of the possibility of rapid confirmation of data in crisis situations, is a situation that needs to be changed and adapted to changing technological and IT capabilities in this area. It seems reasonable to allow registration applications to be submitted electronically using the e-PUAP platform, which will also enable fast and reliable creation of the database of yachts and boats. Undoubtedly, it will be convenient for all to introduce one model registration document and one application for registration together with the exchange of existing paper documents for new plastic cards. It should also be stressed that as regards the proposed amendments, in accordance with Article. 4 registration of a vessel shall be made by any registration authority with which the owner makes an appropriate application (at the seat of the body or in electronic form). The whole registration process will therefore take place in the old age or sports union. This is of great importance due to the existing rights and habits of users. The largest number of vessels is registered in 380 old towns in Poland. The owners of yachts and boats choose registration in the old towns due to a simple and quick procedure, low costs and easy access to the registration point near the place of residence. The changes are not revolutionary here, they merely harmonise the system and allow both owners and state services, including rescue workers, to benefit from rapid data verification. The question arises as to what will be explained in the course of further parliamentary work, what is the cost of building the ICT system and what savings are associated with the proposed changes. The Civic Platform Club welcomes the draft submitted and is in favour of referring it to further parliamentary work.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u394	Member, please. All right, all right, all right, all right, all right, all right, all I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr. Paweł Cybulski to answer questions addressed to the government.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u395	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I would like to say at the outset that tax reduction is not a magic wand that will solve all our problems. It is not true that tax throws Poles abroad, this state of taxation or this level of taxation is the reason that Poles go abroad. It seems to me that these are quite populist phrases, which should not be applied in this case at all. High Izbo! The Parliamentary bill presented before the High Chamber provides for changes in personal income tax which, according to the Ministry of Finance, do not deserve approval. The project proposes a reduction in the uniform rate of personal income tax, i.e. so-called. linear tax from 19% to 15% for small taxpayers, i.e. taxable persons whose value of sales revenue, together with the amount of tax due on goods and services, did not exceed in the previous tax year the amount in PLN corresponding to the equivalent of EUR 1,2 million and taxable persons entering into business in the tax year in which they started business. It cannot be accepted by the projectors that the justification for the proposed amendment is valid from 1 January 2017 in the Corporate Income Tax Act, a 15% tax rate for small taxpayers and start-ups. In view of the existing differences in the tax rules of natural persons engaged in economic activities and of legal persons, it is unreasonable to compare the taxation of these two groups of taxable persons. Individuals active in economic activity already have the possibility to choose the most advantageous form of taxation: in addition to taxation on general principles, according to the tax scale 18% and 32% or at a rate of 19 per cent of the tax may benefit from simplified flat-rate forms of taxation, i.e. a lump sum from registered income or a tax card. The possibility of choosing a simplified form of taxation does not exist for corporate tax taxpayers. It should also be borne in mind that the essence of corporate income tax implies that there is no tax on personal income, i.e. two levels of taxation. taxation of the taxpayer's income, e.g. limited liability companies and then distribution of this tax to shareholders. When assessing the proposed amendments in the parliamentary draft law, it should be stressed that any solutions contained therein are directed, inter alia. to taxable persons entering into business, but the taxable person entering into business is not defined, which in practice means that any taxable person who is a natural person who is currently established and who benefits from taxation at a uniform rate 19% when starting the same activity on the day following its liquidation, it will be entitled to benefit from a 15% tax rate. In fact, there is no solution in the Parliamentary draft law, which would exclude the possibility of applying a 15% tax rate, e.g. by taxable persons entering into business who, in the course of a tax year or in the previous year, carried on their activities themselves or as partners of a non-legal company. The financial impact - perhaps briefly on this issue - is a reduction of revenue of public finance units in the first year of the year by PLN 4.5 billion, assuming that the proposed change will apply to all taxpayers currently clearing income at a uniform rate of 19%. For small taxpayers, the result would be about PLN 2.6 billion in the first year. This effect may be different, since it cannot be excluded that the introduction of a 15% tax rate will result in some taxable persons having income tax on individuals engaged in a flat-rate activity on income recorded or a tax card opting for this form of taxation as being more favourable, but there is no way to determine the financial consequences in this respect. It is also difficult to agree with the assessment of the designers that the cost of the project will be financed by additional resources collected by the government as a result of the announced sealing of the VAT system and the increased tax revenue due to the improved performance of PIT clearing companies as a result of the reduction of the tax rate. The above sources of funding for the loss of revenue by local government units may prove to be purely hypothetical, since no specific mechanism supported by legislation has been presented which would allow for compensation for lost revenue for local government units. In addition, it should be noted that local government units do not participate in VAT revenue, as Mrs Masłowska said earlier, and therefore the potential increase in VAT revenues would only compensate for the loss of revenue of the state budget. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The project assumes the exemption from PIT of a pension or a survivor's pension from social security paid by ZUS or KRUS. This means that these benefits need to be netted and the amount of the lowest pension - PLN 1000 - adjusted by the amount of tax on PIT. The project includes preferential taxation of income for single parents, rehabilitation relief, relief for children. In their explanatory memorandum, the projecters are concerned that netisation is not a source of savings for public finances, and therefore they propose a special compensation benefit set on the same terms as relief for PIT taxpayers, except that payment of benefits will be carried out by the Social Security Office and the KRUS. Projectors themselves indicate that the netisation of pension benefits will result in a decrease in the income of local government units. Projectors in the explanatory memorandum of the bill fear that incentives to work may not have any positive effect and may not induce any additional pensioner to earn money, but can only raise the income of those who already supplement their income with benefits from work. The projectors themselves claim that netisation may worsen the financial situation of public benefit organisations which benefit from contributions from pensioners. In view of all these concerns, in a less effective way, and the complex and indistinct authors of the draft law propose minor solutions to create a legal mess. According to the opinion of the Social Security Office, the compensation provided for in the draft law is not a social security provision and will therefore not be subject to the provisions concerning the coordination of social security systems. This will raise doubts in the context of the application of the provisions of the Article. OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 671. 1 pension law on the choice of payment of a higher or chosen benefit, e.g. in the case of a confluence of a unetted survivor's pension with an ununetted invalidity pension. Proposed provision in Article. 6 point 1 of the netisation project does not contain an algorithm for reproducing the amount of the benefit. In addition, this provision takes away the possibility of deduction for the refund of unduly collected benefits which previously increased taxable income, in amounts that take account of the tax collected. ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u407	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I would like to present a statement on the draft law amending the Personal Income Tax Act and some other laws, print No 2122. The project concerns the exemption from PIT of a pension or a survivor's pension from social security paid by KRUS or ZUS, which, according to the justification, means that these benefits must be settled and that the amount of the lowest pension should be adjusted by the amount of the PIT tax. The project includes preferential income taxation for single parents, rehabilitation relief and relief for children. To pensioners who would be entitled to these benefits in the case of income taxation, a special compensation benefit will be granted on the same terms as the relief for PIT taxpayers and the payment of benefits will be implemented by the CSO and the CRUs. As the projectors point out in the explanatory memorandum, these provisions will increase the activity of older people and the taking up of survivors' pensions. The importance of this social group's activity will grow with the ageing of Polish society. The proposed solutions will compensate for the effective taxation of PIT for older people and other people's work. In addition, tax reductions will be maintained, i.e. rehabilitation relief and relief for children. The care allowance will be increased to PLN 250 and the tax write-off will increase from 1 to 1.5% of the input tax. However, the bill raises some doubts. The estimated cost of the state budget for the implementation of the proposed measures will be about PLN 1 billion. This is a huge amount in view of the current state of public finances and the listed deficit. So the question arises as to whether its deepening should take place. Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In view of the above, the Posel Club Modern is in favour of further work on this bill. Activation of older people should take place. Activating older people is a priority for Poland. In the perspective of many years with an ageing society, this is a priority, so it is worth discussing, it is worth talking about. But, of course, the question remains, at what cost. I hope that during the work on this bill, the authors of the project will answer this question. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The level of poverty of older people is increasing, who are not only not able to afford a decent life but who also often live below the poverty line. The bill under investigation will have a positive impact on the financial situation of the beneficiaries of pensions. The implementation of this project will result in a significant reduction in revenue from personal income tax to the State budget. The PIT tax is an important source of revenue for local government units, so the project assumes a refund from the state budget to local governments lost revenue from this source. The project provides that the share of the local government unit in income tax revenues from individuals, from the taxable persons of that tax resident in the area of the competent entity, must not be less than 102% the amount of the previous tax year. The issue of the refund of lost income and its amount requires a broad discussion at a later stage of legislative work, as we believe that the growth in income of local government units from PITs is higher than 2% per year. The adoption of the bill will not only improve the material situation of pensioners and pensioners, which must be clearly emphasised, but will also have a positive impact on their professional activity. Given the growing problems in the labour market, increasing the activity of pensioners is very much needed and expected. This is all the more justified by the fact that many pensioners are interested in continuing their professional activities. You just need encouragement. The draft act submitted to the Parliament is justified on social and economic grounds and deserves to be addressed to the Committee on Social Policy and the Family, as well as to the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, in order to further process. ParlaMint-PL_2018-03-06-sejm-59-4.u455	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I would like to say that I would be honoured to present to the High House the draft official position of the Council of Ministers prepared in the Ministry of Family, Work and Social Policy. Here at the same time, I would like to point out that I am Vice Minister of the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy. One of the Members asked where the minister was, so I answer that question. He is in the courtroom and has a draft position of the Council of Ministers prepared. It can be said that in this draft position we draw attention to two types of reservations about the solutions proposed in the draft law discussed today. These are legislative reservations and, on the other hand, reservations on the financial implications of the proposed regulation. As far as legislative reservations are concerned, let me be honest, the title of the law or the draft law itself raises reservations. The State has titled this bill: the Law on State aid to seniors, on the amendment of the Act on personal income tax, on the amendment of certain other laws. I would like to point out that this is a project which involves exempting from the obligation to pay income tax on individuals not only pensioners - I understand that, when talking about seniors, you mean people who have reached retirement age - but also provides for this kind of relief applied to pensioners, persons who have been declared unfit for work, or persons who have acquired the right to a survivor's pension. I don't think these people fit into the concept of "senior." There are a number of errors when it comes to quoting the laws that you are proposing to amend in this project, e.g. The Act on the Retirement Provision of Officers of 18 February 1994 does not take into account the fact that the provisions of this Act are also covered by the Customs Service, Customs and Customs Service. It can be said that these categories of persons are also covered by the pension provision provided for in this Act. Mr Kosiniak-Kamish began his speech with such an advertisement of this project, claiming that it is a project that implements the idea of universality. If I were Mr President, I would be very careful with these thesis, because it is precisely the problem that a number of categories of people receiving pensions have been left out in this project. You skipped out on this project, for example. persons collecting structural pensions in agriculture as provided for in the Act of 26 April 2001 To be honest, for a party representing the people's movement, I think that's not something to be proud of. [[At second reading, we would have improved it.]] You have also left out pension recipients under the Act of 10 December 1993 on pension provision for professional soldiers and their families. You've left out people taking social benefits. In our opinion, we also point out that people receiving pensions and foreign pensions have been left out. The authors of the project - here I quote the draft position of the Council of Ministers - do not explain the reasons why they made such a choice of pensions and pensions, i.e. why a tax exemption would apply only to certain pensions and some would remain in the personal income tax system. It's hard to judge whether the authors did it consciously or whether it was their mistake. Here I would like to quote another part of this position: It is necessary to divide the opinion of the projectors that the entry into force of the proposed changes would have a positive impact on the financial situation of the beneficiaries of pensions, especially since the project's authors propose that benefits be waived and paid grossly. However, it should be noted that, in the introduction in 1992 of personal income tax and thus the taxation of pensions and pensions, all national pensions, pensions and wages for work were grossed out, i.e. increased by the amount of tax due in such a way that, after deduction of the tax, the benefit paid is not lower than the benefit which was due prior to the introduction of the income tax. Here it is also worth paying attention to the reservation regarding systemality. This thread appeared in the discussion. As laid down in the Act of 13 October 1998 on the social security scheme paid in the course of the tax year directly to the taxpayer's own pension, sickness and accident insurance and to persons cooperating with it or deducted in the tax year by the taxpayer, contributions shall be deducted from the income which forms the basis for income tax on individuals. In short, we do not pay income tax on due contributions to pension, pension, sickness and accident insurance. Therefore, since the amounts of social security contributions are deductible from the tax base, it is justified and fair to be subject to taxation of benefits resulting from those contributions, i.e. pensions. It is also doubtful whether all pensioners and pensioners should benefit from such tax relief. Here the question arises as to those who receive relatively high pension benefits. We refer in the draft position of the Council of Ministers to data published on the Bankier portal. pl, from which it appears that 75 thousand. pensioners receive over 5 000 benefits from the Social Insurance Institution. Gross. It is therefore reasonable to ask whether, in the case of benefits, as I said, relatively high income tax exemptions from individuals should be used. The State in the explanatory memorandum to the bill refers to similar solutions that operate in some countries of the European Union, Bulgaria, Hungary and Lithuania. We note that PIT, personal income tax, unlike goods and services tax, is not harmonised within the European Union. The individual Member States of the European Union are free to formulate legal arrangements for this tax, as well as to decide on taxation, reliefs or exemptions. The level of personal income tax varies from one Member State to another and the tax base varies from one Member State to another. As far as legislative shortcomings are concerned, I think it is worth noting that you are proposing to put new legal solutions in chapter 10 of the Personal Income Tax Act, i.e. in the part of the Act containing transitional and final provisions. These are rules which, by definition, are temporary or incidental. The subject matter of taxation, as well as the exemption of pensions, the possible exemption of pensions and pensions from personal income tax should be regulated in the substantive and legal part of that law, in particular in the article. 21 Act on personal income tax. This is an important thing when it comes to maintaining the existing systematics of the Personal Income Tax Act. Speaking of some legislative chaos that such proposals could create, Mr Gosiewiski certainly also had in mind proposals that would disrupt the structure of the Personal Income Tax Act. The Parliamentary project - this is the conclusion as regards the legislative objections to this project - in relation to the amendment of the Personal Income Tax Act is underdeveloped, internally contradictory. For this reason, we propose in the draft position to negatively critically assess these solutions. It would also have a negative effect on the proposals made in this project regarding the revenue of the public finance sector. On the basis of the data resulting from the 2016 tax returns, the financial impact of introducing this solution by reducing the revenues of the public finance sector from personal income tax would be about PLN 15 billion. This is the amount that is close to the amount contained in the explanatory memorandum to the bill. It can be said that the proposal to introduce a mechanism to provide individuals with income tax revenues from natural persons of 102% The revenue from the year preceding loses the idea of dividing the revenue from this tax between the state budget and the local government unit. Such a significant loss at the level of PLN 15 billion cannot be left without affecting the revenues of local government units. The third element that includes this draft law is the proposal that people who collect pensions be exempted from health insurance contributions as well. It is worth paying attention to the non-systemicity and inconsistencies of this regulation. That is to date, the Act on public-funded health benefits has consistently implemented the principle that the right to public-funded health benefits is linked to the obligation to pay health insurance contributions. This is also the case for those who do not earn any income - here I will mention the example of unemployed people who do not receive unemployment benefits. In cases where there is an obligation to be subject to health insurance and the insured person does not achieve income, the burden of covering the costs of the health insurance contribution shall be borne by the State budget. The designer does not exempt these persons from health insurance, but only provides that they will be exempt from the obligation to pay their health insurance contributions. Such a solution today in the Public Health Benefits Act simply does not exist. There is no exemption from the obligation to pay health insurance contributions. With regard to the loss of funds or losses in respect of income from the National Health Fund for non-payment of health insurance contributions from pensioners and pensioners, this loss in 2018 we estimate... Here I will add the revenue from health insurance contributions collected by the Social Insurance Institution and the Agricultural Insurance Fund...

On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the parliamentary draft law on State aid to seniors, on the amendment of the Act on personal income tax and on the amendment of certain other laws, print No 2182. Retired people have been a social group that has long been overlooked and often forgotten by politicians. Today, the PiS rules, and I regret to say that all you do is determine when we should retire. But this isn't Senior Care. You gave them a chance to be free early, considering that once they're retired, they're gonna be okay. The important thing is that they get what they want. And they're doing just fine. But that is not the point of Poles' dreams of dignified old age. The point is that we politicians should strive to ensure that our seniors - regardless of their retirement age - have decent, peaceful and prosperous old age, because it is the future of our parents, ours and our children. And it is hard to claim dignity when you receive a minimum pension, which from 1 March is PLN 1029,80 gross. It is even harder to live for a net amount than this pension, so for less than PLN 880. This pension is almost 260 thousand. women, while 1/3 pensioners get between PLN 1000 and PLN 1600. On the other hand, men most often get a pension in the range of PLN 2800 gross. Given how expensive life is and how high bonuses and rewards are given to the governing team, it must be acknowledged that the lowest pension is only sufficient for the Prime Minister's cleansing swabs. Where's the meds, the apartment fees, the money for rehabilitation and candy for grandkids? Imagine the dignity of your parents if in the pharmacy, buying a prescription, they were guided by how much they could spend on drugs, not by what drugs they had to buy to keep their health healthy. Did you happen to hear in line at the pharmacy: Master, I have 50 PLN. Which prescription is the most important? 'Cause I heard it. And it was humiliating not only for this person, but also for me, citizens of the state who allow it. To change that, it's worth doing anything. For these reasons, this law is a step in the right direction. The piss buddies showed that they know how to give away money. I'll give you that: you're generous. There is no social responsibility, there is no equality of rights. You give children who have parents who are capable and ready to work. You take away the rencistoms and pensioners, which are most often lonely, ill, disabled people, who, due to their age and poor health, are unable to take care of their own most urgent needs. For this to be low, you have reduced the retirement age by increasing the number of recipients of the lowest benefits. According to ZUS data by 13 October 2017, pension decisions were issued for nearly 100,000. persons who have applied for a reduction in the retirement age. In fact, the taxation of pensions is the second taxation of our money - our lifelong contributions in the form of compulsory contributions now make up our pension, and our pension is cut again by further amounts. Modern is in favour of the solution proposed by PSL, but we are concerned that our budget, already very strained, will endure. For here we are talking about 28 billion PLN, because this bill is going to cost so much every year. This is a reduction in revenue to the state budget for personal income tax of about PLN 15 billion. About 13 billion PLN will reduce the level of health insurance contributions. The projectors want to compensate for the increased impact on taxes on goods and services. In our sense, this cannot be done, for example, because of a fraction of the profit in relation to the amount spent. A different source of funding should therefore be sought. I hope the government can help with that. We will support the proposal and proceed to work on this law in committee, proposing our amendments.

According to the World Health Organisation, PM10's mean daily concentration level is 50 Sz/m3 and 25 Sz (25 Sz) for more dangerous PM2.5 dust. How do other Member States deal with this problem? The Italians declare an alarm at level 75, France - at level 80, and Hungarians and Czechs, when the concentration level reaches 100 Sz/m3. In Poland, information about smog is given when the level exceeds 200, while the alarm is announced when it reaches 300. Interestingly, these levels were amended by the Regulation of the Minister of the Environment in 2012. I understand it wasn't your rule, but you took power over two years ago. Why don't you just restore the previous level? It wouldn't be much, but it would be a step in the right direction. Another thing is that these levels and alarms are not respected even with such high standards. I checked the level of air pollution in Mielec a few days ago around noon and I was shocked. Pulse suspended PM2,5 - level 183, Pulse MP10.425 Pulse/m3. Kronospan is poisoning the air in Mielec and you can see it with the naked eye. The company promised to reduce the emissions of harmful substances but failed to meet the deadlines twice in 2017. Therefore, I am not surprised to the residents that they do not believe in the company's next declaration that in May 2018 it will reduce the emissions of harmful substances. The Marshal's Office of the Podkarpackie Voivodeship issued a permanent monitoring decision on the highest chimney in the company, but Kronospan appealed against this decision and it was your ministry that agreed to this appeal, claiming that such monitoring was not required by law. Minister, the law is one thing, but the health and safety of Polish citizens is most important to me. I don't know about you, but for me, the Natura 2000 will never be more important, the protection of fish, birds and others, and in particular the protection of the interests of certain companies, if the health and safety of Poles are at risk. By accepting Kronospan's cancellation, you stated that continuous measurement is not required by law. I have nothing left but to quote the senior marshal that the law that does not serve the citizens is lawlessness. Another thing is that Kronospan has dozens of chimneys, it will soon be 39. Even if we set up continuous monitoring on one of them, e.g. on the highest, it doesn't change anything, because harmful substances can be secreted by other chimneys. On the other hand, if we lower the alarm levels, we will give some tools to the local government, which, when responding to smog alarms, will be able to take decisive steps and counteract air pollution in Mielec. Therefore, Minister, on behalf of the inhabitants of Mielec and the Kukiz club15, I ask for help so that the inhabitants are not left alone in this fight. Mielec's authorities are uphill, too. There was an appointment at Kronospan from the city office, but after President Kozdęba's death, the arrangements concerning this visit were broken. Once again, I ask for your help and special attention to be paid to the problem of air quality in Mielec. Thank you very much.
